The combined company rebranded as \"Tebra\" and completed its brand unification in April 2024 when the PatientPop website was fully integrated into tebra.com.\n     9→\n    10→Tebra positions itself as the \"only all-in-one EHR+ platform built exclusively for independent healthcare practices.\" The company serves over **140,000 private healthcare providers** and manages over **125 million patient records**. At the time of the merger, the combined company had approximately 1,000 employees. In December 2025, Tebra secured **$250 million** in new equity and debt financing (led by Hildred, with J.P. Morgan providing the debt facility), over-subscribed with participation from existing investors Toba Capital, Transformation Capital, and HLM Venture Partners. A prior $72 million round had reportedly pushed the company's valuation above $1 billion. The company's target market is squarely **ambulatory independent/private practices** across a range of specialties including primary care, mental health, pediatrics, dermatology, chiropractic, podiatry, and others. It also serves billing companies that manage multiple practices.\n    11→\n    12→## Product: Tebra EHR (EHR+ Platform)\n    13→\n    14→CHPL ID: 11557\n    15→\n    16→### What It Is\n    17→\n    18→Tebra EHR is a cloud-based, all-in-one practice management and electronic health records platform designed for independent and small-to-mid-size ambulatory practices. The certified module is \"Tebra EHR\" (v5.1, certified December 19, 2024), but it is part of a broader integrated platform the company brands as \"EHR+\" — which encompasses clinical documentation (EHR), billing and revenue cycle management, patient experience/engagement, practice marketing, and AI-powered automation tools, all accessible through \"one login, one platform.\"\n    19→\n    20→The product's lineage traces to **Kareo Clinical** (the EHR component) and **Kareo Billing** (the practice management/billing component), which were already integrated products before the Tebra rebrand. The PatientPop merger added the practice marketing and patient experience growth tools. Today, Tebra markets the combined platform as a single product suite.\n    21→\n    22→The CHPL certification covers a broad set of criteria: clinical data (a)(1)–(a)(5), (a)(12), (a)(14); transitions of care (b)(1)–(b)(3); EHI export (b)(10)–(b)(11); patient portal/VDT (e)(1), (e)(3); public health — cancer case reporting (f)(5); and FHIR APIs (g)(2)–(g)(7), (g)(9)–(g)(10). Additional software dependencies listed in the certification include **LabSoft** (lab integration), **Updox** (health information exchange/faxing), **DrFirst Rcopia** (e-prescribing), and **SmileCDR** (FHIR server).\n    23→\n    24→### Users & Market\n    25→\n    26→- **Target users**: Ambulatory clinicians (physicians, NPs, PAs), practice managers, billing staff, front-desk staff, and patients (via portal). The CHPL metadata describes intended users as \"Ambulatory clinicians and staff.\"\n    27→- **Settings**: Independent/private practices, solo practitioners, small-to-mid-size group practices, and billing companies. Not targeted at hospitals, health systems, or enterprise organizations.\n    28→- **Specialties**: Primary care, family medicine, internal medicine, mental/behavioral health, pediatrics, dermatology, chiropractic, podiatry, OB/GYN, and others. The product is generalist-ambulatory, not specialty-locked.\n    29→- **Scale**: 140,000+ providers, 125M+ patient records. The company highlights ratings of 4.6/5 on Trustpilot and 4.1/5 on G2, and 3.9/5 on Capterra (1,311 reviews).\n    30→- **Deployment**: Cloud-hosted (SaaS). The billing module has historically required a desktop application download for some functions, which reviewers have noted as a pain point.\n    31→\n    32→### Modules & Functionality\n    33→\n    34→Based on vendor website, feature pages, mandatory disclosures, press releases, and third-party reviews, Tebra's platform includes the following major functional areas:\n    35→\n    36→**Clinical Documentation / EHR**\n    37→- SOAP note charting with pre-built templates, macros, and text shortcuts\n    38→- **AI Note Assist**: ambient AI documentation that reduces note-taking time (vendor claims 60% reduction per note; over 500,000 notes generated by customers). HIPAA-compliant.\n    39→- **SALT** (pull-forward past notes): allows pulling forward prior visit content into new notes\n    40→- Problem lists, medication lists, allergy lists\n    41→- Patient Facesheet showing medications, allergies, complaints, vitals, medical history, and recent lab results\n    42→- Clinical decision support and drug interaction checking\n    43→- Visit summaries and care plans\n    44→\n    45→**E-Prescribing**\n    46→- Powered by **DrFirst Rcopia** (third-party integration)\n    47→- Prescription ordering with drug interaction checks, refill tracking\n    48→- Integrates with **Surescripts** for prescription routing\n    49→\n    50→**Electronic Labs**\n    51→- Lab ordering and results management\n    52→- Bidirectional interfaces with **LabCorp** and **Quest Diagnostics**\n    53→- Integration via **LabSoft**\n    54→- eLab results viewable by patients in the portal\n    55→\n    56→**Telehealth**\n    57→- Built-in HIPAA/HITRUST-certified video visits\n    58→- Group appointments (up to 200 participants)\n    59→- Dedicated provider URLs, virtual waiting rooms\n    60→- No separate login required\n    61→\n    62→**Scheduling & Appointments**\n    63→- Provider scheduling with calendar management\n    64→- Online patient self-scheduling (book or request)\n    65→- Automated appointment reminders via text, email, and phone\n    66→- Two-way text communication with patients\n    67→\n    68→**Patient Intake**\n    69→- Digital intake forms completed online before visits\n    70→- Data flows directly into the EHR to reduce manual entry\n    71→\n    72→**Patient Portal**\n    73→- 24/7 patient access to visit summaries, test results, lab results, medications, medical history\n    74→- Secure two-way messaging between patients and providers\n    75→- Treatment plan viewing and e-signing\n    76→- Patient education materials and assessments\n    77→- Bill viewing, online payments, and transaction history\n    78→- Guest/caregiver access authorization\n    79→- Document sharing (send and receive health records)\n    80→\n    81→**Billing & Revenue Cycle Management**\n    82→- Insurance eligibility verification in real-time via ANSI X12 270/271 queries to 2,700+ payers\n    83→- Electronic claim submission with automatic error detection, payer-specific rules, and batch processing\n    84→- ERA/EOB auto-posting via RPA (robotic process automation)\n    85→- Denial tracking and management\n    86→- Automated patient statements (paper and electronic) with QR codes\n    87→- Patient payment collection: card-on-file, online, in-person, email/text payment links\n    88→- Accounts receivable tracking and reporting\n    89→- Customizable dashboards for denials, reimbursements, and collections\n    90→- Charge capture and coding support\n    91→- The billing module originated as **Kareo Billing** and some reviewers note it requires a desktop application for certain billing functions\n    92→\n    93→**Practice Marketing** (from PatientPop lineage)\n    94→- Custom practice websites with SEO optimization\n    95→- Paid search marketing management\n    96→- Online reputation management: review monitoring across platforms, AI Review Replies, AI Review Insights\n    97→- Social media marketing\n    98→- Directory profile management across 80+ platforms\n    99→\n   100→**Reporting & Analytics**\n   101→- Quality dashboard for MIPS reporting and tracking\n   102→- Medicare Promoting Interoperability monitoring\n   103→- Customizable billing/revenue dashboards\n   104→- Automated MIPS reporting (no additional documentation entry required per vendor)\n   105→\n   106→**Interoperability & Data Exchange**\n   107→- C-CDA document generation and exchange (transitions of care)\n   108→- FHIR R4 APIs (certified under (g)(10))\n   109→- Health information exchange via **Updox**\n   110→- Cancer case reporting (f)(5)\n   111→- EHI export capability: C-CDA, PDF, and native document formats (per mandatory disclosures)\n   112→\n   113→### Data & Content\n   114→\n   115→Based on the features described above, the Tebra platform stores and manages EHI for individual patients and their patient population at any time without developer assistance\" in C-CDA, PDF, and native document formats.\n   173→- Third-party reviews on Capterra note that data export/migration away from Tebra can be costly (\"potentially costing thousands just to obtain your own data\"), suggesting the EHI export process may have practical friction despite regulatory requirements.\n   174→- The reliance on third-party components (DrFirst for e-prescribing, LabSoft for labs, Updox for HIE, SmileCDR for FHIR) means some data may flow through or be stored in these partner systems. Whether EHI export covers data held by these integrated systems is an important question for Phase 2 analysis.\n   175→- The billing module's desktop application requirement (noted by reviewers) suggests a potentially different data architecture for billing vs. clinical data.\n   176→- The website does not mention imaging/radiology ordering or results as a distinct feature, though it is referenced in passing on one page (\"Order prescriptions, lab tests, and imaging from one platform\"). It also serves billing companies that manage multiple practices.\n\n## Product: Tebra EHR (EHR+ Platform)\n\nCHPL ID: 11557\n\n### What It Is\n\nTebra EHR is a cloud-based, all-in-one practice management and electronic health records platform designed for independent and small-to-mid-size ambulatory practices. The certified module is \"Tebra EHR\" (v5.1, certified December 19, 2024), but it is part of a broader integrated platform the company brands as \"EHR+\" — which encompasses clinical documentation (EHR), billing and revenue cycle management, patient experience/engagement, practice marketing, and AI-powered automation tools, all accessible through \"one login, one platform.\"\n\nThe product's lineage traces to **Kareo Clinical** (the EHR component) and **Kareo Billing** (the practice management/billing component), which were already integrated products before the Tebra rebrand. The PatientPop merger added the practice marketing and patient experience growth tools. Today, Tebra markets the combined platform as a single product suite.\n\nThe CHPL certification covers a broad set of criteria: clinical data (a)(1)–(a)(5), (a)(12), (a)(14); transitions of care (b)(1)–(b)(3); EHI export (b)(10)–(b)(11); patient portal/VDT (e)(1), (e)(3); public health — cancer case reporting (f)(5); and FHIR APIs (g)(2)–(g)(7), (g)(9)–(g)(10). Additional software dependencies listed in the certification include **LabSoft** (lab integration), **Updox** (health information exchange/faxing), **DrFirst Rcopia** (e-prescribing), and **SmileCDR** (FHIR server).\n\n### Users & Market\n\n- **Target users**: Ambulatory clinicians (physicians, NPs, PAs), practice managers, billing staff, front-desk staff, and patients (via portal). The CHPL metadata describes intended users as \"Ambulatory clinicians and staff.\"\n- **Settings**: Independent/private practices, solo practitioners, small-to-mid-size group practices, and billing companies. Not targeted at hospitals, health systems, or enterprise organizations.\n- **Specialties**: Primary care, family medicine, internal medicine, mental/behavioral health, pediatrics, dermatology, chiropractic, podiatry, OB/GYN, and others. The product is generalist-ambulatory, not specialty-locked.\n- **Scale**: 140,000+ providers, 125M+ patient records. The company highlights ratings of 4.6/5 on Trustpilot and 4.1/5 on G2, and 3.9/5 on Capterra (1,311 reviews).\n- **Deployment**: Cloud-hosted (SaaS). The billing module has historically required a desktop application download for some functions, which reviewers have noted as a pain point.\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nBased on vendor website, feature pages, mandatory disclosures, press releases, and third-party reviews, Tebra's platform includes the following major functional areas:\n\n**Clinical Documentation / EHR**\n- SOAP note charting with pre-built templates, macros, and text shortcuts\n- **AI Note Assist**: ambient AI documentation that reduces note-taking time (vendor claims 60% reduction per note; over 500,000 notes generated by customers). HIPAA-compliant.\n- **SALT** (pull-forward past notes): allows pulling forward prior visit content into new notes\n- Problem lists, medication lists, allergy lists\n- Patient Facesheet showing medications, allergies, complaints, vitals, medical history, and recent lab results\n- Clinical decision support and drug interaction checking\n- Visit summaries and care plans\n\n**E-Prescribing**\n- Powered by **DrFirst Rcopia** (third-party integration)\n- Prescription ordering with drug interaction checks, refill tracking\n- Integrates with **Surescripts** for prescription routing\n\n**Electronic Labs**\n- Lab ordering and results management\n- Bidirectional interfaces with **LabCorp** and **Quest Diagnostics**\n- Integration via **LabSoft**\n- eLab results viewable by patients in the portal\n\n**Telehealth**\n- Built-in HIPAA/HITRUST-certified video visits\n- Group appointments (up to 200 participants)\n- Dedicated provider URLs, virtual waiting rooms\n- No separate login required\n\n**Scheduling & Appointments**\n- Provider scheduling with calendar management\n- Online patient self-scheduling (book or request)\n- Automated appointment reminders via text, email, and phone\n- Two-way text communication with patients\n\n**Patient Intake**\n- Digital intake forms completed online before visits\n- Data flows directly into the EHR to reduce manual entry\n\n**Patient Portal**\n- 24/7 patient access to visit summaries, test results, lab results, medications, medical history\n- Secure two-way messaging between patients and providers\n- Treatment plan viewing and e-signing\n- Patient education materials and assessments\n- Bill viewing, online payments, and transaction history\n- Guest/caregiver access authorization\n- Document sharing (send and receive health records)\n\n**Billing & Revenue Cycle Management**\n- Insurance eligibility verification in real-time via ANSI X12 270/271 queries to 2,700+ payers\n- Electronic claim submission with automatic error detection, payer-specific rules, and batch processing\n- ERA/EOB auto-posting via RPA (robotic process automation)\n- Denial tracking and management\n- Automated patient statements (paper and electronic) with QR codes\n- Patient payment collection: card-on-file, online, in-person, email/text payment links\n- Accounts receivable tracking and reporting\n- Customizable dashboards for denials, reimbursements, and collections\n- Charge capture and coding support\n- The billing module originated as **Kareo Billing** and some reviewers note it requires a desktop application for certain billing functions\n\n**Practice Marketing** (from PatientPop lineage)\n- Custom practice websites with SEO optimization\n- Paid search marketing management\n- Online reputation management: review monitoring across platforms, AI Review Replies, AI Review Insights\n- Social media marketing\n- Directory profile management across 80+ platforms\n\n**Reporting & Analytics**\n- exchange via **Updox**\n- Cancer case reporting (f)(5)\n- EHI export capability: C-CDA, PDF, and native document formats (per mandatory disclosures)\n\n### Data & Content\n\nBased on the features For groups with fewer than 15 participants or if you are in a rural or health professional shortage area, the requirement is to complete up to two activities for a minimum of 90 days. If you are in a certified patient-centered medical home, you automatically get full credit.</p>\n\n </div>\n </div>\n </li>\n  <li class=\"accordion-item hidden-question hidden\" data-accordion-item>\n <a href=\"#\" class=\"flex accordion-title\">\n <div class=\"grid-x full-width\">\n <div class=\"cell small-10\">\n <h3 class=\"heading h6\"> How many improvement activities are there to choose from?</h3>\n </div>\n </div>\n </a>\n <div class=\"accordion-content\" data-tab-content>\n <div class=\"body-text regular medium\">\n <p>There are over 100 improvement activities to choose from.</p>\n\n </div>\n </div>\n </li>\n  <li class=\"accordion-item hidden-question hidden\" data-accordion-item>\n <a href=\"#\" class=\"flex accordion-title\">\n <div class=\"grid-x full-width\">\n <div class=\"cell small-10\">\n <h3 class=\"heading h6\">What is the Promoting Interoperability requirement for MIPS?</h3>\n </div>\n </div>\n </a>\n <div class=\"accordion-content\" data-tab-content>\n <div class=\"body-text regular medium\">\n <p>The Promoting Interoperability category emphasizes the electronic exchange of information. There are five objectives &#8211; Protect Patient Health Information, ePrescribing, Health Information Exchange, Provider to Patient Exchange, and Public Health and Clinical Data Exchange. Each objective has its own requirements. These objectives must be measured for a minimum of 90 consecutive days in the calendar year.</p>\n\n </div>\n </div>\n </li>\n  <li class=\"accordion-item hidden-question hidden\" data-accordion-item>\n <a href=\"#\" class=\"flex accordion-title\">\n <div class=\"grid-x full-width\">\n <div class=\"cell small-10\">\n <h3 class=\"heading h6\">How will we report our data for MIPS?</h3>\n </div>\n </div>\n </a>\n <div class=\"accordion-content\" data-tab-content>\n <div class=\"body-text regular medium\">\n <p>Providers need to register on the qpp.cms.gov website. Once registered, results from the Promoting Interoperability category, the Improvement Activities, and Quality Measures can be submitted. The cost category is automatically collected from Medicare claims. Alternative methods of submitting for Quality measures are available and are described at qpp.cms.gov.</p>\n\n </div>\n </div>\n </li>\n  <li class=\"accordion-item hidden-question hidden\" data-accordion-item>\n <a href=\"#\" class=\"flex accordion-title\">\n <div class=\"grid-x full-width\">\n <div class=\"cell small-10\">\n <h3 class=\"heading h6\">Is Medicare Advantage included or does this just apply to Medicare?</h3>\n </div>\n </div>\n </a>\n <div class=\"accordion-content\" data-tab-content>\n <div class=\"body-text regular medium\">\n <p>This program only applies to Medicare Part B.</p>\n\n </div>\n </div>\n </li>\n  <li class=\"accordion-item hidden-question hidden\" data-accordion-item>\n <a href=\"#\" class=\"flex accordion-title\">\n <div class=\"grid-x full-width\">\n <div class=\"cell small-10\">\n <h3 class=\"heading h6\">Can the required data for MIPS be captured using Tebra Clinical?</h3>\n </div>\n </div>\n </a>\n <div class=\"accordion-content\" data-tab-content>\n <div class=\"body-text regular medium\">\n <p>Yes, Tebra Clinical provides the fields necessary to capture data from both the Quality and the Promoting Interoperability categories. Clinicians seeking to report quality measures not supported by Tebra may still submit any measure in their specialty set when submitted via claims throughout the reporting period.</p>\n\n </div>\n </div>\n </li>\n  </ul>\n  <div class=\"cta-wrapper flex-container\">\n <button class=\"btn secondary rounded\" id=\"has-more-btn\">Show more</button>\n </div>\n  </div>\n </div>\n </div>\n</section>\n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n  \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n\n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n  <section class=\"custom-content\">\n <section id=\"certification\" class=\"desc2 grid-container\">\n    <div class=\"lockup-cont\">\n        <div class=\"logos\">\n            <img class=\"\" src=\"/wp-content/uploads/2024/08/EHR-CB-073a-ONC-Certification-Drummond-Combined-Logo.png\"\n                style=\"max-width: 100%\">\n            <img class=\"\" src=\"/wp-content/uploads/2024/08/EHR-CB-084a-Drummond-ONC-Health-IT-Certified-Seal.png\"\n                style=\"max-width: 100%\">\n        </div>\n        <h3 class=\"\">ONC Health IT Certification</h3>\n        <p class=\"\">This Health IT Module is compliant with the ONC Certification Criteria for Health IT and has been\n            certified by an ONC-ACB in accordance with the applicable certification criteria adopted by the Secretary of\n            Health and Human Services. The export encompasses patients whose clinical notes were created, signed, co-signed, or assigned by/to selected providers during specified timeframes, along with associated patient documents.\n\n## Accessing the Export Feature\n\nUsers navigate through:\n1. User icon → Practice Settings\n2. Data Management section\n3. Export Patient Data option\n\nThis opens the Export Patient Summaries page, displaying exports from the last 60 days.\n\n## One-Time Export Process\n\nThe Run Export dialog requires:\n- **Date Range**: Selection period for patient visits\n- **Provider Selection**: All providers or specific individual\n- **Export Timing**: Either immediate (\"Now\") or scheduled for a specific date/time\n\nAfter configuration, the export appears in the Patient Summaries list with \"Pending\" status.\n\n## Recurring Export Process\n\nUsers can automate exports by:\n- Selecting frequency (Monthly on the 1st or Weekly on Mondays)\n- Choosing provider(s)\n- Managing recurring exports via trash icon deletion\n\n## Download and File Access\n\nWhen ready, export status changes to \"Ready\" and initiating users receive notification through the Message Center. The U.S. Core Data for Interoperability (USCDI) v3 defines EHI more specifically, encompassing:\n\n- Current clinical data set elements\n- Eight clinical note categories: \"history and physicals, progress notes, procedure notes, consult notes, imaging reports, laboratory reports, pathology reports, and discharge summaries\"\n\nNote: Initially, information blocking requirements applied only to USCDI-defined EHI, which excludes billing records despite the broader definition.\n\n## Data Export Methods and Processes\n\nTebra provides patient access to EHI through multiple mechanisms:\n\n1. **Patient Portal**: Patients with portal access can view USCDI-defined EHI\n2. **Alternative Export Methods**: For other EHI categories, \"Tebra can provide access through other means\" including \"a secure ShareFile service through which exports of EHI may be shared\"\n3. **Request Process**: For information not available in the patient portal, individuals should \"submit a request for EHI using the Cures Act Request Form\"\n\n## Compliance Framework\n\nThe FAQ notes Tebra \"allows the sharing of health information through our patient portal\" as part of the ONC Health IT Certification Program and indicates the organization will \"develop additional processes to facilitate EHI access, exchange, and use\" as regulations evolve."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"2ab0f106-9631-417e-9e9e-1d4a58e9a771","uuid":"981ebaab-a186-4747-84c7-3e666943dd36","tool_use_result":{"bytes":69282,"code":200,"codeText":"OK","result":"# 21st Century Cures Act: EHI Export and Data Sharing\n\n## What Constitutes Electronic Health Information (EHI)\n\nAccording to the FAQ, EHI includes \"all medical and billing records about a patient\" based on HIPAA's designated record set concept. Here are the key findings:\n\n## Data Format Requirements\n\nThe export files must be electronic and in a computable format. However, ONC requires that EHI be exported in an electronic, \"machine-readable\" format but it doesn't require a particular format, merely that documentation describing the format \"must be made available through a publicly accessible hyperlink\".\n\n## Common Format Standards\n\nThe two common formats that seem relevant are Consolidated-Clinical Document Architecture (C-CDA) and FHIR®. ONC doesn't require either one and even mentions .CSV files as an acceptable option.\n\nSeveral vendors implement specific formats:\n- Legend EHR uses the HL7 Consolidated Clinical Document Architecture (C-CDA) Release 2.1\n- CliniComp's FHIR server supports FHIR Bulk Data EHI Export for patient population and supports bulk data export downloads in C-CDA 2.1 XML format\n- OpenEMR's Electronic Health Information Export files are contained inside a zip folder where each zip contains a batch of 1 or more patients\n\n## Documentation Requirements\n\nHealth IT developers must attest they have a process for keeping the export format(s) used to support this section up-to-date.\n\nThe search results did not return specific information about Tebra's implementation of b(10) EHI export. The specific provisions include:\"\n      uid=2_48 StaticText \"Prohibit health IT developers from participating in any action that constitutes information blocking;\"\n      uid=2_49 StaticText \"Bar health IT developers from prohibiting or restricting communications about EHI related to certain subjects; and\"\n      uid=2_50 StaticText \"Require API developers and their technology to promote an open and competitive marketplace.\"\n      uid=2_51 StaticText \"Where can I find more information about compliance with the Cures Act?\"\n      uid=2_52 StaticText \"Many physician associations (e.g., \"\n      uid=2_53 link \"AMA \" description=\"https://www.ama-assn.org/\" url=\"https://www.ama-assn.org/\"\n        uid=2_54 StaticText \"AMA \"\n      uid=2_55 StaticText \"and \"\n      uid=2_56 link \"MGMA\" description=\"https://www.mgma.com/\" url=\"https://www.mgma.com/\"\n        uid=2_57 StaticText \"MGMA\"\n      uid=2_58 StaticText \") publicly provide additional information on compliance with the Cures Act.\"\n      uid=2_59 StaticText \"What is information blocking?\"\n      uid=2_60 StaticText \"Information blocking is defined as:\"\n      uid=2_61 LineBreak \"\n\"\n      uid=2_62 LineBreak \"\n\"\n      uid=2_63 StaticText \"A practice by a healthcare provider, health IT developer of certified health information technology, or health information network (HIN)/health information exchange (HIE) that is likely to interfere with access, exchange, or use of electronic health information (EHI), except as required by law or specified by the Secretary of Health and Human Services (HHA) as a reasonable and necessary activity.\"\n      uid=2_64 LineBreak \"\n\"\n      uid=2_65 LineBreak \"\n\"\n      uid=2_66 StaticText \"The rule should have been named “Information Sharing” because the goal is for patients to have access to their information. For exclusions to sharing, review the \"\n      uid=2_67 link \"exception FAQ\" description=\"21st Century Cures Act FAQs\" url=\"https://helpme.tebra.com/CMS_Incentive_Programs/21st_Century_Cures_Act/21st_Century_Cures_Act_FAQs#exceptions\"\n        uid=2_68 StaticText \"exception FAQ\"\n      uid=2_69 StaticText \".\"\n      uid=2_70 StaticText \"What information constitutes electronic health information (EHI)?\"\n      uid=2_71 StaticText \"The Cures Act requires EHI to be shared electronically with patients. The definition of EHI comes from, but is slightly broader than, the HIPAA concept of a “designated record set,” which includes all medical and billing records about a patient. However, initially, the information blocking requirements of the Cures Act only related to EHI as defined by the United States Core Data for Interoperability (USCDI). Currently, the USCDI definition of EHI includes only specific medical information, not billing records. In addition to the elements in the current Common Clinical Data Set (CCDS) which serves as the foundation of the Summary of Care and Referrals used by physicians to transmit patient data, USCDI has recently announced that EHI also includes the following clinical note categories: (1) history and physicals, (2) progress notes, (3) procedure notes, (4) consult notes, (5) imaging reports, (6) laboratory reports, (7) pathology reports, and (8) discharge summaries (typically found in hospital settings).\"\n      uid=2_72 StaticText \"Tebra has updated its application to capture all the data elements required by USCDI v3and .meets the requirements of the ONC Health IT Certification Program.\"\n      uid=2_73 StaticText \"USCDI v3 adds two additional data classes and 20 data elements. \"\n      uid=2_74 image \"CuresAct_USCDIv3.png\" url=\"https://helpme.tebra.com/@api/deki/files/13168/CuresAct_USCDIv3.png?revision=2\"\n      uid=2_75 StaticText \"As a provider, do I have to make all EHI available to my patients?\"\n      uid=2_76 StaticText \"While there is no requirement for providers to make EHI available to patients who have not requested it, a delay in the release or availability of EHI in response to a request for legally permissible access to or exchange or use of EHI may be considered an interference. In addition, providers need to be aware of the HIPAA obligations to provide patients with access to their records.\"\n      uid=2_77 StaticText \"Is Tebra software compliant with the requirements of the Cures Act?\"\n      uid=2_78 StaticText \"As part of the ONC Health IT Certification Program, Tebra allows the sharing of health information through our patient portal. If a patient has access to Tebra’s patient portal, he or she has access to the EHI defined by the USCDI, For other categories of EHI, Tebra can provide access through other means. Tebra  utilizes other technologies to provide patient access to EHI, including a secure ShareFile service through which exports of EHI may be shared.\"\n      uid=2_79 LineBreak \"\n\"\n      uid=2_80 LineBreak \"\n\"\n      uid=2_81 StaticText \"As requirements under the Cures Act continue to evolve, Tebra will develop additional processes to facilitate EHI access, exchange, and use.\"\n      uid=2_82 StaticText \"Where can I submit a request for EHI, if the information I am looking for is not in my patient portal?\"\n      uid=2_83 StaticText \"Please submit a request for EHI using the \"\n      uid=2_84 link \"Cures Act Request Form\" description=\"https://www.tebra.com/cures-act-request/\" url=\"https://www.tebra.com/cures-act-request/\"\n        uid=2_85 StaticText \"Cures Act Request Form\"\n      uid=2_86 StaticText \".\"\n      uid=2_87 StaticText \"Can third parties request access to patients’ EHI?\"\n      uid=2_88 StaticText \"The Cures Act will allow third parties, such as technology vendors or payers seeking patient information, to request EHI on behalf of patients. Future access for such parties will likely occur through an electronic interface. However, providers must still meet the requirements of state laws regarding access to medical records and HIPAA’s patient right of access obligations.\"\n      uid=2_89 StaticText \"Are there any exceptions to providing a patient or their representative access to EHI?\"\n      uid=2_90 StaticText \"Yes, the Information Blocking Regulations provide for eight (8) exceptions when a physician would not have to provide access to EHI. Those exceptions are:\"\n      uid=2_91 StaticText \"Preventing Harm Exception: It will not be information blocking for an actor to engage in practices that are reasonable and necessary to prevent harm to a patient or another person, provided certain conditions are met.\"\n      uid=2_92 StaticText \"Privacy Exception: It will not be information blocking if an actor does not fulfill a request to access, exchange, or use EHI in order to protect an individual’s privacy, provided certain conditions are met.\"\n      uid=2_93 StaticText \"Security Exception: It will not be information blocking for an actor to interfere with the access, exchange, or use of EHI in order to protect the security of EHI, provided certain conditions are met.\"\n      uid=2_94 StaticText \"Infeasibility Exception: It will not be information blocking if an actor does not fulfill a request to access, exchange, or use EHI due to the infeasibility of the request, provided certain conditions are met.\"\n      uid=2_95 StaticText \"Health IT Performance Exception: It will not be information blocking for an actor to take reasonable and necessary measures to make health IT temporarily unavailable or to degrade the health IT's performance for the benefit of the overall performance of the health IT, provided certain conditions are met.\"\n      uid=2_96 StaticText \"Content and Manner Exception: It will not be information blocking for an actor to limit the content of its response to a request to access, exchange, or use EHI or the manner in which it fulfills a request to access, exchange, or use EHI, provided certain conditions are met.\"\n      uid=2_97 StaticText \"Fees Exception: It will not be information blocking for an actor to charge fees, including fees that result in a reasonable profit margin, for accessing, exchanging, or using EHI, provided certain conditions are met.\"\n      uid=2_98 StaticText \"Licensing Exception: It will not be information blocking for an actor to license interoperability elements for EHI to be accessed, exchanged, or used, provided certain conditions are met.\"\n      uid=2_99 StaticText \"How do I know if an exception applies?\"\n      uid=2_100 StaticText \"Physicians may want to have written policies that help them know how to appropriately respond to requests for EHI. To the extent the physician is relying on the EHR to implement the access requests, the physician should work with Tebra to set up electronic rules that can be followed. For example, flagging/indicating which type of EHI should not be shared with patients (e.g., psychotherapy notes).\"\n      uid=2_101 StaticText \"What happens if I don’t comply with the Information Blocking Regulations?\"\n      uid=2_102 StaticText \"The Cures Act provides for significant penalties for healthcare providers, vendors and HIE/HIN parties that fail to comply with the prohibition on information blocking. The OIG has the authority to enforce these regulations, reviewing complaints of non-compliance on an individual basis.\"\n      uid=2_103 StaticText \"Do I have to verify my compliance with the Information Blocking Regulations?\"\n      uid=2_104 StaticText \"Maybe. If you are participating in the Merit-based Incentive Payment System, you will be required to attest that you:\"\n      uid=2_105 StaticText \"Did not knowingly or willfully limit or restrict the compatibility or interoperability of certified EHR technology;\"\n      uid=2_106 StaticText \"Implemented technologies, standards, and policies reasonably calculated to ensure that the EHR technology conforms with applicable health IT requirements and specifications; and\"\n      uid=2_107 StaticText \"Responded in good faith to requests for EHI.\"\n      uid=2_108 link \"Back to top\" enacted for a variety of reasons across the care continuum. The Cures Act fosters innovation in health care technology to deliver better information while promoting visibility into the services, quality, and costs of health care.\"\n      uid=2_45 LineBreak \"\n\"\n      uid=2_46 LineBreak \"\n\"\n      uid=2_47 StaticText \"The two rules that are important to Tebra’s customers are (1) Implementing Interoperability and (2) Information Blocking. WordPress site on WP Engine behind Cloudflare. Content-Type: text/html.\n\n### Step 2: Page examination\nThe registered URL is a MACRA/MIPS marketing and compliance page titled \"Quality programs & regulatory support.\" The page contains:\n- MACRA/MIPS marketing content and product screenshots\n- FAQ accordion about MACRA/MIPS (not EHI-related)\n- ONC Health IT Certification section with vendor info, certification details, and certified modules listing\n- Real World Test Plans table (2022-2025)\n- APIs section linking to two PDFs: FHIR API Documentation and General Clinical API Documentation\n- **EHI Export Functionality section** — a single paragraph (the entirety of the (b)(10) export documentation)\n\n### Step 3: EHI Export section content (verbatim)\nFound at the bottom of the certification section (HTML lines 2425-2431):\n> **EHI Export Functionality**\n> Tebra EHR enables providers to export out EHI for individual patients and their patient population at any time without developer assistance. Export formats include C-CDA documents for clinical records, PDFs for claims and billing information, and also documents in the original native format which they were uploaded.\n\nNo links to further documentation, data dictionary, schema, sample files, or format specifications.\n\n### Step 4: Downloaded linked documents\nFrom the page, I downloaded:\n- FHIR API User Guide PDF (May 2025): `https://www.tebra.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/05/Tebra-FHIR-API-User-Guide.pdf`\n- General Clinical API Documentation PDF (2023): `https://www.tebra.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/10/General_API_Documentation-Tebra.pdf`\n- Costs and Guidance PDF (Dec 2022): `https://learn.tebra.com/rs/677-NCQ-300/images/TB-Costs-and-Guidance-Dec-2022.pdf`\n- Real World Test Plans and Results (2022-2025)\n- Full page HTML and screenshot\n\n### Step 5: Searched help center\nSearched `helpme.tebra.com` for EHI export documentation via web search and direct browsing. Key findings:\n- **Export Patient Clinical Data** (`https://helpme.tebra.com/Platform/Practice_Settings/Data_Management/Export_Patient_Clinical_Data`): Describes how to do a bulk export of patient clinical data as individual XML Summary of Care (.ccda) files, filterable by date range and provider. One-time or recurring exports. Downloaded as a zip file.\n- **21st Century Cures Act FAQs** (`https://helpme.tebra.com/CMS_Incentive_Programs/21st_Century_Cures_Act/21st_Century_Cures_Act_FAQs`): Describes EHI scope, notes that USCDI-defined EHI is available via patient portal, and for other EHI categories \"Tebra can provide access through other means\" including \"a secure ShareFile service through which exports of EHI may be shared.\" Directs patients to submit a \"Cures Act Request Form.\"\n\n### Step 6: Found Cures Act Request Form\nThe FAQ links to `https://www.tebra.com/cures-act-request/` — a OneTrust-powered privacy portal form. The form allows patients (or authorized agents) to request specific categories of EHI:\n\n1. Allergies and Intolerances\n2. Assessment and Plan of Treatment\n3. Care Team Members\n4. Clinical Notes\n5. Immunizations\n6. Laboratory Tests & Results\n7. Medications\n8. Patient Demographics\n9. Problems\n10. Procedures\n11. Patient's Implantable Device(s)\n12. Vital Signs\n13. Past Medical History\n14. Past Surgical History\n15. Family History\n16. Social History (e.g., smoking status)\n17. OB/Pregnancy History\n18. Account/Insurance Information\n19. Documents\n\n### Step 7: Examined Real World Test Results\nThe 2024 Real World Test Results (315(b)(10)) document actual EHI export usage:\n- 755 providers tested over 3 months (April-June 2024)\n- 3,931 total EHI exports performed\n- 790 practices performing exports\n- ~4.98 exports per practice average\n- Most exports contained fewer than 100 patients (short date ranges)\n- Majority contained 10 or fewer patients (single-day or two-day range)\n- All exports completed without issue\n- Export includes C-CDAs and \"other patient documents\"\n\n### Step 8: Searched for additional documentation\n```\nWeb search: tebra EHR \"EHI export\" data dictionary format documentation 2024 2025\nWeb search: site:helpme.tebra.com \"EHI export\" OR \"electronic health information export\"\n```\nNo additional EHI export-specific documentation found. No data dictionary, schema, or format specification exists beyond what is described above.\n\n## What Was Found\n\n### EHI Export Documentation Summary\n\nTebra's EHI export documentation is **extremely minimal**. The entire (b)(10) export documentation on the registered URL is a single paragraph that describes three export formats:\n\n1. **C-CDA documents** for clinical records\n2. **PDFs** for claims and billing information\n3. **Native format documents** (documents in the format they were uploaded)\n\nThere is no data dictionary, no schema definition, no field-level documentation, no sample export files, and no format specification of any kind.\n\n### Export Mechanism\n\nPiecing together information from the help center and test results, the EHI export works as follows:\n\n**For clinical data (C-CDA export):**\n- Accessible via Practice Settings > Data Management > Export Patient Data\n- Exports individual XML Summary of Care files (.ccda extension) per patient\n- Filterable by date range and provider\n- Can be one-time or recurring (monthly on 1st or weekly on Mondays)\n- Downloads as a zip file\n- Now also includes uploaded patient documents (added November 2023)\n\n**For billing data:**\n- Exported as PDFs (per the mandatory disclosures paragraph)\n- The practice management module (formerly Kareo Billing) has separate report exports for demographics, charges, unpaid claims, A/R aging, and fee schedules in Excel/CSV format\n\n**For other EHI categories:**\n- Tebra provides a \"Cures Act Request Form\" (OneTrust privacy portal) where patients can request 19 categories of data\n- Fulfillment is via \"a secure ShareFile service\"\n- This is a manual, patient-initiated request process rather than a self-service export feature\n\n### FHIR API (g(10), not b(10))\n\nThe FHIR API User Guide (May 2025) documents a SmileCDR-powered FHIR R4 API for USCDI v1 data. It covers:\n- Standard US Core resource types: Patient, AllergyIntolerance, CarePlan, CareTeam, Condition, DiagnosticReport, DocumentReference, Encounter, Goal, Immunization, Implantable Device, Laboratory Result Observation, Location, Medication, MedicationRequest, Observation, Organization, Practitioner, PractitionerRole, Procedure, Provenance\n- OAuth 2.0 authentication (3-legged for patient apps, 2-legged for backend services)\n- Backend bulk data transfers for authorized applications\n\nThis is explicitly the (g)(10) standardized API, not the (b)(10) export. The guide itself states it covers \"USCDI version 1 requirements\" and uses \"HL7 FHIR US Core Implementation Guide STU3 Release 3.1.1.\"\n\n### General Clinical API\n\nAn older (2023) patient-facing API that provides JSON access to clinical data via API key authentication through the patient portal. Also covers only clinical data — not a (b)(10) mechanism.\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nBased on the product research, Tebra stores data across clinical, billing, scheduling, telehealth, patient portal, and practice marketing domains. Here is the coverage assessment:\n\n**Clearly covered (via C-CDA export):**\n- Patient demographics\n- Problems/conditions\n- Medications\n- Allergies\n- Vital signs\n- Lab results\n- Immunizations\n- Clinical notes (as Summary of Care content)\n- Assessment and plan\n- Procedures\n- Implantable devices\n\n**Partially covered (via PDF or request form):**\n- Billing records — described as \"PDFs for claims and billing information\" but no details on what billing fields are included or excluded\n- Account/Insurance Information — listed as a selectable category on the Cures Act Request Form\n- Documents — uploaded documents now included in the export (since November 2023)\n\n**Ambiguous or likely missing:**\n- Prescription history detail (e-prescribing is handled by DrFirst Rcopia; unclear if Rx history beyond what's in C-CDA is exported)\n- Lab order details (labs are handled by LabSoft; unclear if full order history is exported)\n- Telehealth visit records (metadata, recordings if stored)\n- Patient portal secure messages\n- Patient intake form data\n- Appointment/scheduling data (operational, but visit history linked to encounters may qualify)\n- Referral records (beyond what's in C-CDA transitions of care)\n\n**Clearly not covered (and expected to be):**\n- Detailed billing data at the field level — charges, line items, CPT/ICD codes, ERA/EOB detail, denial information, claim status history, payment records. The \"PDF for claims and billing\" language suggests rendered summaries rather than computable billing data.\n- Custom clinical forms/templates — Tebra supports SOAP note templates and macros; the data captured in these custom fields is unlikely to be fully represented in a standard C-CDA\n- AI Note Assist generated content — the product touts AI-generated notes; unclear if the underlying data (audio transcripts, AI processing metadata) is included\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe export uses three formats:\n\n1. **C-CDA (XML)** — A recognized interoperability standard, appropriate for clinical data. However, C-CDA is a summary document standard, not a comprehensive database export. It captures the most recent/active clinical state well but is not designed to capture the full history of billing transactions, custom form data, or system-specific fields.\n\n2. **PDF** — For claims and billing. PDF is not a computable format. A developer cannot programmatically parse PDF billing records to reconstruct billing history. This is a significant limitation for (b)(10) compliance, which requires data in a \"computable\" format.\n\n3. **Native format** — Documents in their original upload format. Appropriate for documents but doesn't address structured data.\n\nThe mismatch between format and requirement is notable: the (b)(10) requirement specifies export in \"computable\" format. C-CDA satisfies this for clinical data, but PDF does not satisfy it for billing data. A CSV or JSON export of billing records would be more appropriate.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Very poor.** The documentation quality is among the weakest possible for a certified product:\n\n- **No data dictionary whatsoever** — There is no listing of tables, fields, data types, or value sets anywhere in the publicly accessible documentation\n- **No schema or format specification** — No XSD, JSON Schema, or even a prose description of what fields are in the C-CDA or billing PDF exports\n- **No sample export files** — No examples of what an exported C-CDA looks like or what a billing PDF contains\n- **No field-level definitions** — Nothing beyond \"C-CDA documents for clinical records, PDFs for claims and billing\"\n- **No export instructions** — The mandatory disclosures page has no link to the help center article that actually describes how to perform the export\n- **No version history or changelog** — No indication of how the export has evolved\n\nThe FHIR API guide is relatively well-documented (33 pages with resource descriptions, field tables, authentication flows), but it documents the (g)(10) API, not the (b)(10) export.\n\nA developer attempting to import Tebra EHI export data would have essentially no documentation to work with beyond the C-CDA standard itself.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n- **Granularity**: Table/field level documentation is completely absent for the (b)(10) export\n- **Value sets**: Not documented for the export. The FHIR API guide references standard terminologies (SNOMED, ICD-10, LOINC, RxNorm) but these apply to the (g)(10) API, not the (b)(10) export\n- **Relationships**: Not documented\n- **Versioning**: No version history. The costs_and_guidance PDF still references \"Kareo EHR\" (the pre-rebrand name) and cites (b)(6) rather than (b)(10), suggesting it predates the December 2024 certification\n- **Completeness**: The Cures Act Request Form lists 19 categories but there is no documentation of what data is included in each category or what format the response takes\n\n### The (b)(10) vs (g)(10) Distinction\n\nTebra presents a nuanced but ultimately inadequate picture. The mandatory disclosures paragraph correctly distinguishes the EHI export (which includes billing PDFs and native documents) from the FHIR API (which covers USCDI clinical data). This shows awareness that (b)(10) is broader than (g)(10).\n\nHowever, the clinical data portion of the EHI export is a C-CDA — which is essentially the same Summary of Care document used for transitions of care under (b)(1)/(b)(6). The 2024 test plan originally measured \"Patient Batch Exports\" under (b)(6) and then retroactively substituted it for the \"broader 315(b)(10) EHI Export\" — suggesting the same export mechanism was relabeled. The C-CDA Summary of Care covers roughly the same data as USCDI/US Core, so the clinical portion of the EHI export provides similar coverage to the (g)(10) API.\n\nThe key distinction Tebra makes is adding billing PDFs and uploaded documents to the export. This is a step beyond pure (g)(10), but the PDF format for billing data undermines the computable format requirement, and there is no evidence of comprehensive export of specialty-specific clinical data, custom forms, or structured billing records beyond what fits in a C-CDA and a rendered PDF.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://www.tebra.com/tebra-resources/macra-mips\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no (all content accessible via curl; browser used for screenshots and to examine Cures Act Request Form iframe)\n- Navigation complexity: direct_link (EHI section is inline on the registered page)\n- Anti-bot issues: none (Cloudflare present but no blocking)\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n\n- The Costs and Guidance PDF is outdated (December 2022) and references \"Kareo EHR\" with (b)(6) certification criteria rather than (b)(10). It does not mention EHI export.\n- The FHIR API User Guide and General API Documentation are well-documented but cover (g)(10) patient API access, not (b)(10) EHI export.\n- No downloadable data dictionary, schema, or format specification exists for the EHI export.\n- Web searches for Tebra EHI export documentation returned no results beyond what was found on the registered URL and help center.\n- The help center article \"Export Patient Clinical Data\" describes the mechanics of running a C-CDA batch export but provides no details about what data fields are included in the export.\n- The Cures Act Request Form (OneTrust privacy portal) is a manual patient request process rather than technical documentation of export format.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":407,"cache_read_input_tokens":118813,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":407},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"2ab0f106-9631-417e-9e9e-1d4a58e9a771","uuid":"d0dd8c2e-1047-4ab7-9bfc-0cdfa4c61c77"}

The guide itself states it covers \"USCDI version 1 requirements\" and uses \"HL7 FHIR US Core Implementation Guide STU3 Release 3.1.1.\"\n\n### General Clinical API\n\nAn older (2023) patient-facing API that provides JSON access to clinical data via API key authentication through the patient portal. Also covers only clinical data — not a (b)(10) mechanism.\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nBased on the product research, Tebra stores data across clinical, billing, scheduling, telehealth, patient portal, and practice marketing domains. Here is the coverage assessment:\n\n**Clearly covered (via C-CDA export):**\n- Patient demographics\n- Problems/conditions\n- Medications\n- Allergies\n- Vital signs\n- Lab results\n- Immunizations\n- Clinical notes (as Summary of Care content)\n- Assessment and plan\n- Procedures\n- Implantable devices\n\n**Partially covered (via PDF or request form):**\n- Billing records — described as \"PDFs for claims and billing information\" but no details on what billing fields are included or excluded\n- Account/Insurance Information — listed as a selectable category on the Cures Act Request Form\n- Documents — uploaded documents now included in the export (since November 2023)\n\n**Ambiguous or likely missing:**\n- Prescription history detail (e-prescribing is handled by DrFirst Rcopia; unclear if Rx history beyond what's in C-CDA is exported)\n- Lab order details (labs are handled by LabSoft; unclear if full order history is exported)\n- Telehealth visit records (metadata, recordings if stored)\n- Patient portal secure messages\n- Patient intake form data\n- Appointment/scheduling data (operational, but visit history linked to encounters may qualify)\n- Referral records (beyond what's in C-CDA transitions of care)\n\n**Clearly not covered (and expected to be):**\n- Detailed billing data at the field level — charges, line items, CPT/ICD codes, ERA/EOB detail, denial information, claim status history, payment records. The \"PDF for claims and billing\" language suggests rendered summaries rather than computable billing data.\n- Custom clinical forms/templates — Tebra supports SOAP note templates and macros; the data captured in these custom fields is unlikely to be fully represented in a standard C-CDA\n- AI Note Assist generated content — the product touts AI-generated notes; unclear if the underlying data (audio transcripts, AI processing metadata) is included\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe export uses three formats:\n\n1. **C-CDA (XML)** — A recognized interoperability standard, appropriate for clinical data. However, C-CDA is a summary document standard, not a comprehensive database export. It captures the most recent/active clinical state well but is not designed to capture the full history of billing transactions, custom form data, or system-specific fields.\n\n2. **PDF** — For claims and billing. PDF is not a computable format. A developer cannot programmatically parse PDF billing records to reconstruct billing history. This is a significant limitation for (b)(10) compliance, which requires data in a \"computable\" format.\n\n3. **Native format** — Documents in their original upload format. Appropriate for documents but doesn't address structured data.\n\nThe mismatch between format and requirement is notable: the (b)(10) requirement specifies export in \"computable\" format. C-CDA satisfies this for clinical data, but PDF does not satisfy it for billing data. A CSV or JSON export of billing records would be more appropriate.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Very poor.** The documentation quality is among the weakest possible for a certified product:\n\n- **No data dictionary whatsoever** — There is no listing of tables, fields, data types, or value sets anywhere in the publicly accessible documentation\n- **No schema or format specification** — No XSD, JSON Schema, or even a prose description of what fields are in the C-CDA or billing PDF exports\n- **No sample export files** — No examples of what an exported C-CDA looks like or what a billing PDF contains\n- **No field-level definitions** — Nothing beyond \"C-CDA documents for clinical records, PDFs for claims and billing\"\n- **No export instructions** — The mandatory disclosures page has no link to the help center article that actually describes how to perform the export\n- **No version history or changelog** — No indication of how the export has evolved\n\nThe FHIR API guide is relatively well-documented (33 pages with resource descriptions, field tables, authentication flows), but it documents the (g)(10) API, not the (b)(10) export.\n\nA developer attempting to import Tebra EHI export data would have essentially no documentation to work with beyond the C-CDA standard itself.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n- **Granularity**: Table/field level documentation is completely absent for the (b)(10) export\n- **Value sets**: Not documented for the export. The FHIR API guide references standard terminologies (SNOMED, ICD-10, LOINC, RxNorm) but these apply to the (g)(10) API, not the (b)(10) export\n- **Relationships**: Not documented\n- **Versioning**: No version history. The costs_and_guidance PDF still references \"Kareo EHR\" (the pre-rebrand name) and cites (b)(6) rather than (b)(10), suggesting it predates the December 2024 certification\n- **Completeness**: The Cures Act Request Form lists 19 categories but there is no documentation of what data is included in each category or what format the response takes\n\n### The (b)(10) vs (g)(10) Distinction\n\nTebra presents a nuanced but ultimately inadequate picture. The mandatory disclosures paragraph correctly distinguishes the EHI export (which includes billing PDFs and native documents) from the FHIR API (which covers USCDI clinical data). This shows awareness that (b)(10) is broader than (g)(10).\n\nHowever, the clinical data portion of the EHI export is a C-CDA — which is essentially the same Summary of Care document used for transitions of care under (b)(1)/(b)(6). The 2024 test plan originally measured \"Patient Batch Exports\" under (b)(6) and then retroactively substituted it for the \"broader 315(b)(10) EHI Export\" — suggesting the same export mechanism was relabeled. The C-CDA Summary of Care covers roughly the same data as USCDI/US Core, so the clinical portion of the EHI export provides similar coverage to the (g)(10) API.\n\nThe key distinction Tebra makes is adding billing PDFs and uploaded documents to the export. This is a step beyond pure (g)(10), but the PDF format for billing data undermines the computable format requirement, and there is no evidence of comprehensive export of specialty-specific clinical data, custom forms, or structured billing records beyond what fits in a C-CDA and a rendered PDF.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://www.tebra.com/tebra-resources/macra-mips\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no (all content accessible via curl; browser used for screenshots and to examine Cures Act Request Form iframe)\n- Navigation complexity: direct_link (EHI section is inline on the registered page)\n- Anti-bot issues: none (Cloudflare present but no blocking)\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n\n- The Costs and Guidance PDF is outdated (December 2022) and references \"Kareo EHR\" with (b)(6) certification criteria rather than (b)(10). It does not mention EHI export.\n- The FHIR API User Guide and General API Documentation are well-documented but cover (g)(10) patient API access, not (b)(10) EHI export.\n- No downloadable data dictionary, schema, or format specification exists for the EHI export.\n- Web searches for Tebra EHI export documentation returned no results beyond what was found on the registered URL and help center.\n- The help center article \"Export Patient Clinical Data\" describes the mechanics of running a C-CDA batch export but provides no details about what data fields are included in the export.\n- The Cures Act Request Form (OneTrust privacy portal) is a manual patient request process rather than technical documentation of export format.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
